Title: Hymns and Spiritual Songs, in three books: I. Collected from the Scriptures; II. Compos'd on Divine Subjects; III. Prepar'd for the Lord's Supper; by I. Watts; the ninth edition
Creator: Isaac Watts (1674-1748)
Publisher: London: printed for Richard Ford, at the Angel in the Poultry, near Stocks-Market
Publication Date: 1725
Rights: Public Domain (published 1725, more than 95 years ago; public domain by date under the project's rolling rule; the item carries no rights field, so the date rule alone applies)
Source: Internet Archive item bim_eighteenth-century_hymns-and-spiritual-song_watts-isaac_1725 (https://archive.org/details/bim_eighteenth-century_hymns-and-spiritual-song_watts-isaac_1725), the item's own bim_eighteenth-century_hymns-and-spiritual-song_watts-isaac_1725_djvu.txt full-text OCR derivative; contributing library per the item metadata: Internet Archive; host metadata date: 1725. Fetched directly (curl/urllib, raw bytes) by the Library thread (Era VIII pass), 2026-10-23. sha256 of the raw fetched bytes: 6b2fb6b181b5a3e486344bee5acbe8c0cb0906c4c1620b7dc1ebc3442af3b04d.
Provenance note: Header prepended by the Library thread; nothing else altered, no OCR error corrected, nothing retyped. British Library microfilm scan of an edition printed in Watts's lifetime; readable with the long s kept; some lines pick up a few characters from the facing page.
Content note: The hymn book with which an Independent minister, as the census says, effectively created the English hymn, in three books with Watts's preface on singing in worship; the songs the Dissenting congregations actually sang.

ure, whereby the Verſe is weaken'd and

Criticks: But as my whole Deſign was to

pecially in this Part: And I am ſatisfy'd I 
Mall hereby attain two Ends, (viz.) aſſiſt

be ſung unto God but the Tranſlations of his

the Levitical Ceremonies and Hebrew Forme 
of Speech chang'd into the Worſhip of the 
Goſpel, and explain'd in the Language of 
our Time and Nation; and what would not 
bear ſuch an Alteration is omitted and laid 
aſide. After this manner ſhould I rejoyce |

Comfort under Sorrows and Pains. 


\TOW let the Lord my Saviour ſmile, 
And ſhow my Name upon his Heart, 
| would forget my Pains a-while, 

and in the Pleaſure loſe the Smart. 


But oh! it ſwells my Sorrows high 
To ſee my bleſſed Jeſus frown, 

My Spirits fink, my Comforts die, 
And all the Springs of Life are down. 


Yet why, my Soul, why theſe Complaints ? 
Still while he frowns his Bowels move ; 
Still on his Heart he bears his Saints, 

And feels their Sorrows and his Love. 


My Name is printed on his Breaſt ; 
His Book of Life contains my Name; 
I'd rather have it there impreſt 
Than in the bright Records of Fame. 
When the laſt Fire burns all Things here, 
Thoſe Letters ſhall ſecurely Rand | 

And in the Lamb's fair Book appear 

Writ by th' Eternal Father's Hand. 


Now ſhall my Minutes ſmoothly run, 
Whilſt here I wait my Father's Will : 
My Rifing and my Setting Sun
